Studies show that 35% of cancer cases during childhood are caused by leukemia. In our country, under 16 years of age between 1,200 and 1,500 children are diagnosed with leukemia, but leukemia can be seen at any age. With good treatment and moral support, it is a disease that can be healed at a rate of 85 percent. tbmm-2019-11-06sit04spe0283par0034	Mr. President, we would like to express our views on the Law and the Law Proposal for Changes in Some Laws on Payment and Covenant Systems 117 rows, Payment Services and Electronic Money Corporations. Dear friends, first I'd like to make a brief statement as to why this arrangement is needed. Under Law 6493, which entered into force in 2013, for the first time, our country's legislation created a special arrangement for all payment areas, including payment systems, the security system, the payment service provider, the payment company and the electronic money company. The law, which took effect in 2013, was also assigned to Turkey's Republic Central Bank in connection with the Banking Editor-in-Chief and Control Agency, the payment systems and the security agreement systems that provided payment services and services for these services. The rapid improvements in the area of payments have been necessary to ensure that the world's mechanism of effective decision-making is under one roof of the bilateral regulatory and control mechanism between the Central Bank and the BDDK in a way that is consistent with world practice. This need is the inevitable result of progress in IT and communication technologies and the point where new products and applications in the field of payment come from. As the speakers before me expressed, this is a complete technical arrangement. My dear President, the esteemed MPs, as you can all imagine, today's payments have become one of the cornerstones of macroeconomic stability. At every point that technology has reached, it has become a central banking responsibility as important as the continuous work of the needs available in the field of payment, price stability and financial stability. The main goal here is to maintain price stability and uninterrupted payments. It has also become a necessity to monitor one-handed payment systems, which are a prerequisite for the continuous continuation of economic activities due to unprecedented increases in global and geopolitical risks. In view of all these issues, by offering a change of law, the regulations and auditing of the Banking Organization on the area of payments are being handed over to the Turkish Central Bank of Turkey, provided it has integrity in the law. The speakers before me asked, "Why are they being transferred to the Central Bank? Doesn't the BDK do its duty?" In fact, we'd like to say that the EFT transactions are effective in the Central Bank, so it's an effective approach to getting these transactions into the Central Bank. You are aware that the amendment offer also includes provisions concerning other issues, in addition to the transfer of regulations and auditing powers in the area of the payments of the BDDK. As it is known, the area of payment is a very dynamic sector, so we don't have to run into a brand-new business model in the area of payments. This issue is also mentioned as a diagnosis in international reports. According to international reports, the most affected by the rapid changes in financial technologies is financial services. An arrangement for such rapidly developing and changing sectors in 2013 does not seem possible for the entire sector to meet its needs. As a matter of fact, the talks with actors operating in this area also had serious demands from us to make changes in the current regulations of the sector, which in a sense meets the sector's demands. Before this arrangement was made, meetings were held with sector representatives in the broadest sense. In this context, we've considered the needs of sector players in the area of payment in our proposal for a change of law. I would like to bring to your attention a matter that has been taken into consideration when the changes are being made. As Law 6493 was adopted in 2013, the relevant regulations involved in the European Union's acquisitor were an important factor in considering our country's needs and other good international practices, the European Union's provision for payment services in this area has been changed in 2015. In 2015, the newly added Committee for Payment Services 2 to the European Union's acquisitor was also adapted to it in a sense, given this law. This law is also expected to contribute positively to the development of this area in the areas of payment, where there are organisations operating within this scope. The amendment provisions for starting a payment service and providing account information are also part of our proposal for a different type of payment service, which will be added to Law 6493. Mr. President, valuable lawmakers, as I have just stated, the draft of law proposals for amendments to Law 6493 has been taken into account as well as the needs of changes in the existing regulations, sector demands, international good practices and the precedent of countries, especially the European Union's acquisitor. In this context, we can categorize the provisions of the amendments to the proposal at three main points. One: changes regarding the transfer of the powers in the BDDK to the Central Bank. Two: The Turkish Central Bank of the Republic has been appointed as the authority to organise and supervise the operations of the Post and Telegraph Service for payment services. Changes in the introduction of new payment service definitions to the law in harmony with the world's practice include changes to the law, and a number of exclusion clauses related to starting a payment service in line with many precedent countries and European Union acquistors and providing account information and activities related to these activities are added to Law 6493. Third, other changes added to the draft based on the needs of the law following its legalisation in 2013 are the establishment of Turkey's Payment and Electronic Coins Association as a result of changes to this title. Dear friends, this union is purely professional. So you don't have to look for any other reason, one way or the other. It's entirely based on the demands of professional organizations, or rather the players of this sector. Secondly, we're making an important change. Innovative issues are especially organised, such as the removal of funds invested by money companies in the banks of the Turkish Republic's Central Bank. I would like to point out under all of these issues that the new types of services that have been established in the area of payment, which has a very dynamic structure, in the case of the Law Proposal of Change in Law No. 6493, would be more effective and more proactive, and would be possible to intervene in these needs, including the organisation that provides these services, which has been established in the sector since 2013. Developments in technology increase commercial and financial activities, domestic and international payments as a result of globalisation and liberalisation of financial transactions. In addition to developing technology, various applications in the area of payments are used to pay extra cash for many commercial and financial transactions. Thus, the importance of paying systems that create infrastructure for domestic and international payments is increasing day by day. In 2013, Law 6493 was adopted in order to establish legal infrastructure in the field of payment systems, payment services and electronic money companies. In matters related to this law, the payment and the security system, the Turkish Central Bank of Turkey has been tasked with the Banking Organization and Control Agency on the issues related to payment services and electronic money exporters. The widespread use of payment systems and the possible negative effects of risks from these systems on financial stability highlight the role of central banks in payment systems. The smooth and uninterrupted work of the field of payments has become a matter of the safety of countries, and the stability of the financial system is critical to the monetary policy practices of central banks and the development of the economy. Payment systems allow national and international financial markets to integrate into each other, allowing money authorities to respond to financial shocks on time by facilitating the implementation of monetary policy. In this respect, it is important that all the duties and powers in our country in the area of payment be collected under one roof. Under the law, the Central Bank has been made authority in the area of payment. In this scope, arrangements are made for the transfer of earlier powers to the Banking Editor and Control Agency to the Central Bank, adding new payment definitions, covering new types of services and providing services for these services and completing some of the sector's needs. The National Movement Party supports this arrangement. Dear lawmakers, the Central Bank's main goal is to ensure price stability and financial stability, which is its supporting goal. tbmm-2019-11-06sit05spe0391par0039	Mr. Commission, as you can answer the questions our lawmakers have asked, please come in.
tbmm-2019-11-06sit05spe0393par0040	Sure. Mr. President, I'm going to pass on the questions regarding our agenda, other than the law proposal, to the ministries, and they're going to return to them in writing. Mr. Kusoglu, Mr. Emecan and Mr. Tanal are similar questions. A comprehensive study of Krypto money is conducted within MASAK. The goal of crypto beings is to create an alternative to the payment tools based on the results of the process in traditional financial architecture, based mainly on the money units of the countries, but today it is seen as being available for speculative purposes rather than as an alternative to the technical capacities of many transactions, as a result of the problems related to co-operation in the values of these beings. In this direction, crypto beings are approaching the capital market product rather than a payment tool. International editing examples show that rather than being organised as a payment tool for crypto assets, black money is being tried to be handled within the framework of consumer protection, tax and capital market legislation. In this regard, the approach to giving crypto assets a placement in the area of payment has not been adopted. On the other hand, the transactions carried out by crypto assets appear to be close to an intermediary activity. In the study of international examples, crypto assets are seen to be the main actor's crypto asset stock exchanges, and in the event that this area is organised in our country, these elements are considered to be considered under the legislation on the protection of the consumer. Finally, there appears to be a tendency to adopt similar risk rules, similar rules for those of such crypto beings that continue to debate stable crypto beings in the world, who can enter the market at a global level, to adopt an application in the form of the same rules, but it is considered necessary, given that the study of international institutions is ongoing, that the practice of implementation in this field should not be seen and that international co-operation opportunities should not be considered. That's why we don't have anything to do with the crypto money on this bill. The question of Mr. Yilmaz is: why are the duties of the BDK to the Central Bank in the area of payment? (The concepts such as Payment and Securities Covenant Systems No. 6493, Payment Services and Electronic Money Corporations, which were put into effect in 2013, have been defined for the first time in our country's legislation. In this scope, the Banking Editor-in-Chief and Control Agency had been assigned to provide payment services to customers, or to provide payment services for electronic money exports, as part of the payment providers or other financial institutions, and the Turkish Republic Central Bank had been assigned to conduct transactions between them as members. Basically, due to the rapid change of the sector's dynamics and developing technologies, the need for a bilateral regulation and control mechanism created between the Turkish Central Bank of Turkey and the Banking Organization and the Control Agency on the area of payments has arisen under a single roof. In many countries, such as the Netherlands, Malaysia, Hong Kong, Singapore, Indonesia, China and Russia, all powers for the area of payment are seen under the central bank. The establishment of this kind of structure will first allow for more proactive intervention by identifying the needs that arise in the area of payments that have a very dynamic structure. In addition, the collection of powers under one roof will allow the parties to better analyse and govern their rights and obligations in a market, with a wide range of sector players, such as banks, PTT, and non-bank payment providers, under very difficult conditions of competition among different measures. Again, because of this kind of integration of authority, it will be possible to take action on an sector that is very quickly monitored by major international developments. In this scope, some of the powers belonging to the Banking Editor-in-Chief and Control Agency are given to Turkey's Central Bank to ensure integrity in the law. In his speech on the majority of Mr. Yılmaz and Erol Bey, he asked: "Why is there a need for a separate union when there is a Union of Turkish Banks?" (As it is known, the Union of Banks is an entity where banks can only participate, but there are non-bank organizations operating in the area of payments, and the number of those are more than 50. They are thought to be able to make their voices heard, like other competitors, and to discuss the problems of the industry within themselves. This union is not a trade union, it's actually a professional union. Furthermore, the area of payment is rapidly improving in the world and in our country. In order to adapt and improve the profession in this area, education and research activities, co-ordination and cooperation among sector players are important, and a structure with these activities at stake will facilitate monitoring these developments in the sector.
tbmm-2019-11-06sit05spe0394par0054	Finish your words.
tbmm-2019-11-06sit05spe0393par0055	The union will co-operate in many areas between payment to members and electronic money companies, as well as a guiding role for members. Finally, Mr. Kusoglu has one question left.
tbmm-2019-11-06sit05spe0394par0057	Here, complete that.
tbmm-2019-11-06sit05spe0393par0058	Why are the duties of the BDDK being transferred to the Central Bank? Even though the Law on Payment and Covenant Systems No. 6493 was adopted in 2013, why did it need to change in such a short period of time? Our answer is that because the payment systems and the financial system are dynamic spaces, they are constantly changing technologically and are developing, constantly creating new concepts and applications in these areas. In order to monitor these developments and avoid the emergence of various legal gaps and shortcomings, legal changes and updates must be made at certain intervals. This is happening not only in Turkey, but all over the world. For example, the European Union created its first legislation in the field of payment systems in 2007 under the name "Payment Service Direct" 1. In 2013, it had to make some changes in the law and added new substances to the law. Such changes were not enough, but in 2015, a new law was adopted under the name of "Payment Service Direct" 2ί, but the law failed to follow technological developments, and a further update was made in the law in 2018. As it turns out, the laws passed in this area revealed the need for updates after a while of adoption. Many countries, especially the European Union, have made the necessary changes at intervals. As Turkey, we need these changes to adapt to the current developments and changes, to comply with other countries' legal legislation. Thank you.

tbmm-2019-11-06sit06spe0466par0142	Mr. President, dear lawmakers, I salute you all. I am strongly opposed to this bill as a member of the Marxist economic political team, and I'll try to make a brief explanation of why. In ancient Greece, money had the same etymological origins as cattle. Because the cow was being used as a means of change because there was no money. Later, when one cow, for example, hit 100kg of wheat, the money began to supply step by step under the conditions in which the cow replaced it with another trading vehicle. It was a good means of savings, a good means of enrichment, a good means of exchange. Now, the money's come out, but at that time, different forms of money, different forms of financing, as well as checks, bonds, and other derivative markets. And right now, an electronic money that's going to go beyond money as we know it, is becoming more and more common around the world. The thing about this is, friends, there's a lot of financialization, there's a lot of capital flow going on. With the acceleration of capital flow, the economics of neoliberal economics, especially when the working class was forced to work in a flexible and uncompromising way in working life, the horse's head went all over the world; it's just coming to us; it's just coming to us, it's still coming to us. The term "financialization" means the increase in positive value exploitation over the working class. So the law, in fact, does not benefit the market, the wage, the minimum wage, the standard of living; it will only make a handful of investors, a handful of wealthy people happy. Does it make the economy bigger? Yes, it does. But we socialists look at it this way: the growth of the economy is not something alone. If that economy is creating employment, if it's providing justice for sharing, it's two, and it's not ballooning, it's real growth. It's clear that this law will inflate the economy in a positive sense, yes, but it won't do any practical good to pay, to market inflation, to the poor, to those four people who committed suicide; that's part of the job.
